ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e3952	This answers the first question. We're coming to the 2nd. Question Time, by Mr Deputies Auer will be introduced by his question. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e3973	Mr State Secretary, my question is: 158/M "What are the positive effects on budget planning or on budget planning? - wait for execution Is it through the planned new budgetary law?”
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e3995	Mr State Secretary, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4010	Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of the new budgetary law is to make multiannual planning a regular case. At present, we only have a one-year budget. Multi-annual planning represents only one additional information compared to the one-year planning; it is not geared to accounting. The new major budgetary reform, as has been said, is intended to bring multi-annual planning as a central project. Gender budgeting is also planned. The coordination of public budgets with a view to the European Stability and Economic Pact should be better coordinated. This new law aims to integrate the so-called automatic stabilizers into the law technology fix. That means: If there are economically worse times, there are automatically higher limits of expenditure, and in better economic periods less high. There are also significant improvements for the resorts. You certainly know the famous “December fever. ” Because we only have a one-year budget and free available budgets expire at the end of the year, there are always very many procurements carried out very quickly when free credit is available. This is then no longer necessary, because then freely available resources remain in the resorts and can also be used for new priorities for other purposes. At a further stage of the budgetary reform, we provide for a so-called impact-oriented administration. Each service must then be announced in a service package for which the funds are used. As a compensation there is a so-called global budget. It is no longer part of individual financial legal approaches as before, but it is then available to the entire budget of the respective budget chapter.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4034	An additional question? Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4053	Mr State Secretary, unfortunately there has not yet been an agreement on this new budgetary law. My question is: What is the increased flexibility in the future budget implementation through the new budgetary law?
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4071	Mr State Secretary, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4086	We currently have a lack of time flexibility. After a year, budget resources will be available free of charge. – That’s the one point of view. The second point is the so-called principle of speciality. This means that funds can only be used for the intended purpose, which then expire. It's very difficult to redesign this. One needs either a law to do so, or, if a legal empowerment is provided for in the budget law, the Ministry of Finance will have to be authorised to give an agreement to spend funds for another purpose. All of this can then be decided by the resort itself. We always have a four-year plan, which will be rewritten, and for the next year this will still be specialized. It is an overall framework where every Member can orientate, where the way is going, and yet there is still sufficient flexibility that, if there is a direct occasion, the funds can be diverted for other purposes.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4796	I had the wrong note. – Mrs Minister, my question is [[Congratulation with the SPÖ and the Greens]] 163/M “How has the Austrian population benefited from the measures you have taken in the current legislative period?”
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4823	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4839	Mr Rasinger! High house! Over the last three and a half years, we have ensured, in addition to a major health reform, which for the first time provides for the joint planning and control of the entire Austrian health care system, namely the hospital area and the established area, that the funds used for health care in Austria, more than €14 billion, are planned as efficiently as possible, and that the coordination through a federal health agency and nine national health platforms, in particular, improves the seam points – and I do not deliberately call them “interfaces”. This means that a patient who will be released from the hospital will also be able to find out all that he needs in order to be able to provide for himself at home. This is especially important for older people who have no or only distant relatives. In addition, during this legislative period, we focused on health promotion and health care and, with an evaluation and redesign of the precautionary examination, brought these to the current state of science after 30 years. The Austrians can now be studied free of charge once a year according to the latest findings of the medical science of age-specific, gender-specific. We want to promote the early detection of life-threatening diseases. With this preventive and early detection programme, we want to reduce breast cancer by 30 percent, especially among women. These are at least 1,500 deaths a year, the highest number of cancer deaths in women. In particular, we want to reduce lung cancer rates through a comprehensive non-smoker protection package. There are 3,800 deaths per year, of which 3,400 are directly due to smoking, including passive smoking. In the end, we want to reduce cardiovascular diseases. This is where we have worked with a focus on women's health to find out what science is all about. We have also put in place the health card, the e-card, after many efforts by some of my predecessors. This means that every Austrian/each Austrian needs no more health certificate from the employer/employer. You can use this health card to make a medical visit at any time, also abroad. Within the EU, this card is a substitute for the European health certificate, and beyond the European states, the current scheme applies. Thirdly, we have achieved that patients no longer need to enter the health insurance fund for the authorisation of a medicinal product, but that this authorisation is now possible via the ABS, the pharmaceutical authorisation system, electronically between the doctor and social security. This also ensures that the patient has this approval very quickly and that the unnecessary passage to a chief physician, which he usually did not see himself, remains separate from where a secretary has sent him either a yes or a no. We have also extended the scope of insurance, in particular to those in need of assistance and protection, who have been included in social health insurance, which is very important for asylum seekers, for migrants. We have expanded the list of occupational diseases, and we have, in particular, reformed and significantly improved the calculation of weekly benefits. This will also take greater account of child care benefits. A "equivalent" clause for emergency aid workers has also been introduced.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e4945	Mr Rasinger! High house! I refer, in particular, to the full participation rights they have obtained in the national health platforms and in the Federal Health Agency, as well as to the reduction of red tape. The health card replaces a total of 42 million health certificates, which not only had to be issued by the economy, but also completed by the doctors, billed by the doctors, then had to be settled in the health insurance fund. It's all gone now. We have also drawn up a new refund code for medicinal products. This refund code provides that not, as before, around five million prescriptions are required annually, but only more than one million recipes. This means a reduction of 80 percent and thus also a reduction in the number of authorisation procedures. In addition, we have introduced the Retirement Criteria Regulation, especially in order to achieve equality between doctors. This made it easier for female applicants to have access to the decisive hearing by a contracting authority. We have increased midwives training and training for medical and technical services on a technical level and have thus achieved that they are also europak compatible. We have created specialist schools for health professionals for the gap between compulsory school and care diploma training. We have, above all, one thing, and this is also a matter that is particularly important for women: We have enabled part-time training in care and diploma training, in particular a training for nursing science students and re-primary women, for which I think this has been a very important step. In order to address the lack of qualified care staff, we have made it possible to train nurses, care assistants and qualified staff – usually in the majority of women – to work alongside them. This also creates a higher income for women and at the same time achieves a closure of income shears.

December last year and the second part with the structural quality criteria and the authorisation matrix on 28 December. June of this year. It has succeeded in reaching an agreement between all nine federal states and the Federal Government, so that it is now ensured that the national health platforms, together with the countries responsible for the hospitals, are being discussed, planned and controlled so as to ensure that the hospitals, social insurance companies, the Federal Government, all specialist doctors or the specialist care systems are discussed, planned and managed, thus avoiding, on the one hand, oversupply, and at the same time, a gap in those areas where there is not sufficient specialist care. The Austrian Health Structure Plan also holds out the time within which a particular doctor must be reached. That is, the general physician, the family doctor must be reachable in a radius of 15 minutes. The basic supply also takes 15 to 30 minutes, including internal and surgical care. An ophthalmologist can be up to an hour, a quarter of an hour away, in the remotest regions, since you do not go to the ophthalmologist very often, while you need basic care very quickly. There is a precise definition here, including a need clarification. This can be found in this structure plan, and the countries now have the opportunity to decide on their national health platform where a specialist office is going, where an ambulance is going, where a medical community, a medical society, may be going.

And if these are exceeded, then objective action must be taken. There is also this regulatory authority for the types of measures to be taken. ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e11061	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! I should like to point out that, through this change of law today and by this novel, Mrs Moser, there will be no increase in noise thresholds, as you are constantly saying! This argument is purely apprehension, is the usual alarmism, which is always exercised here by the Greens. Rather, it is a question of making it possible for airports in general to set up anti-aircraft measures in the case of objects of affected pilots and thus to reach a positive conclusion of an EIA procedure, which we need for this third road and to which we also confess, ladies and gentlemen. [[Abg. Dr. Gabriela Moser: Why don't you write it in here?]] We are committed to both the third and the necessary EIA procedures. The aim of our request is precisely to allow for airport projects an approval under the EIA law at all. You know, airports are currently considered to be operating facilities, which may only receive an EIA permit if the effects of the plant are mitigated by measures at the source of emissions. But this is very limited at an airport, and in the case of an aircraft which is the source of noise, noise protection can be ensured only by ongoing technological development. This is also happening, ladies and gentlemen, but in Austria we have no significant influence with our noise protection legislation. But we can ensure that, for example, noise protection measures reduce those affected, and this change of law makes it possible for EIA to be approved when noise protection measures are applied to those affected. These are measures such as the installation of soundproof windows and the like. If certain emission thresholds are exceeded, these measures must be taken in the case of these affected homes and houses. From which thresholds such measures are now foreseen, it is necessary, after an in-depth examination by experts, by experts, by an opinion by means of a corresponding regulation, to be adopted by the Minister of Transport in accordance with the Minister for the Environment. [[Abg. Dr. Gabriela Moser: You would have had years to do this! Six years!]] This has to be done by means of a regulation, Mrs Colleague, so that we can take sufficient account of the results of the mediation procedure here. [[Abg. Dr. Gabriela Moser: There are results! Write them in!]] We can't just write in 65 dB or 55 dB, if we don't know what the mediation procedure looked like in the individual project and what results it came to. [[Abg. Dr. Gabriela Moser: I can present it to you! We got it!]] It is also a difference whether one makes such a noise regulation at a regional airport or at a large airport in the middle of a large city. Of course, we have to react differently here, and that is why we have come to this solution with the regulation. That was right and necessary, and I think that will be possible to confirm and support, above all, those who have been involved in this mediation process for many years. I would like to stress once again that the Vienna airport has expressly stated that it is also on the results of this mediation procedure, and all that has been implemented there will then essentially also be included in the regulation. [[Abg. Dr. Gabriela Moser: Yes, essentially!]] Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that a good, right solution has been found here, a solution that also takes into account individual and regional differences, and that there has been no other more meaningful solution in this context. [[Abg. May. Hakl: (in the direction of Abg. Dr Gabriela Moser) What do you propose instead of a regulation, Mrs Colleague?]] Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e11243	Mrs Hlavac is now speaking. Desirable time: 2 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e11266	Madam President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I would just like to add a few sentences to the German Federal Law on Safety Measures in the case of aircraft from third countries. This is about the implementation of an EU directive. It is a question of harmonising the rules for the verification of third-country aircraft landing at airports in the European Union, in particular the question of the collection of information on accidents, disturbances, complaints and so on. This information may be exchanged with the authorities of the other EEA States and Switzerland. It is a question of preliminary inspections, of powers within the framework of these field inspections, in particular access to aircraft and the like. Measures shall also be regulated in the event of a safety risk. These go to flight bans. This is a useful harmonisation, and we will therefore give our approval.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e11735	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The extension of the European aviation area to include the Balkan countries is a very important step towards the integration of these countries into the European aviation partnership. Harmonised rules in Europe, as mentioned by one of my previous speakers, are important for the development of a common air transport market. Air transport plays an essential role not only in the development of the whole region in the interests of consumers and the economy, but also in the political and economic integration of Europe. Ladies and gentlemen! The future internal air transport market will consist of 35 countries with more than 500 million inhabitants. This also creates new market opportunities for the European aviation industry. Air transport between the EU and South-East Europe has increased by 121 percent since 2001. Annual growth of 6 percent is forecast for the coming years. The Convention ensures efficient air traffic control and optimal control of traffic flows, thus preventing congestion of the airspace and increasing safety. We will therefore give our approval to this agreement. Ladies and gentlemen! In addition to free access to the market, freedom of establishment and equal conditions of competition, the Convention also provides for common rules on air and air safety, flight management, social legislation and environmental protection. It is precisely environmental protection, as we have just been able to pursue in the debate, that is an important issue in relation to air transport. The same conditions are an essential element of European policy, as can be seen very clearly in the current data, such as in the case of kerosene taxation or in the case of environmental pollution caused by air transport. Dear ladies and gentlemen! In view of the environmental impact of air transport, the European Parliament has recently adopted a resolution calling for a separate system of emissions of air transport, kerosene taxation and charges on domestic flights. Finding just solutions to the environmental problems caused by air transport without breaking the growth of this key sector will be one of the major challenges of the coming years. In any case, we agree to this agreement. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e15294	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I would like to see the resolution of the National Council tomorrow and therefore the XXII. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: No, not dissolved, he's still working!]] The legislative period, which was also referred to by the honourable Member, indicates that 33 reports with 191 results were submitted by the Court of Auditors during this legislative period. In the last twelve months alone, there have been 13 reports with 53 examination results. [[Abg. Gahr: Careful!]] I would also like to point out that a large number of the Court of Auditors' suggestions and recommendations have actually been implemented, and I would also like to thank you for having been thoroughly discussed in the Committee or, as is the case today, for having been debated in the National Council. [[Congratulations with the liberals – BZÖ and the ÖVP.]] It is therefore gratifying from the Court's point of view that the Court of Auditors' recommendations are heard, because they not only increase the efficiency and effectiveness of administrative action, but also give more attention to economy, efficiency and effectiveness. I believe that the reports on the agenda today are an example of how much has been implemented, but there are enough concerns where the Court of Auditors' recommendations have not been followed with the necessary follow-up and not with the necessary emphasis. I would like to address this further. An important aspect – it has not yet been addressed today, but has budgetary significance, and therefore I am speaking to it in an extra way – is the test result regarding Semmering base tunnel, renovation of the inventory line. They know that by slowing down the necessary procedures or by, as long as possible, failing to take decisions of the highest court by the interested authorities – which have been held by experts in legal opinions – the implementation of the Semmering Base Tunnel has been delayed for two decades. The result is that a €94 million effort has now been incurred, which can be described as almost lost effort. I would like to point out that on 30 June, the Commission adopted a proposal for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to the laws of the Member States relating to the laws of the Member States relating to turnover taxes and to the laws of the Member States relating to turnover taxes. A Council of Ministers decision was adopted in March 2005 setting out the further I can only hope from the Court of Auditors' point of view that everyone will now confess and, accordingly, the project will actually be realized. For the renovation of the inventory line alone, € 205 million must be spent until the use of the Semmering base tunnel from 2020 onwards. So if the project is delayed longer, a further effort of € 83 million is required, and this effort will be lost when the Semmering base tunnel is realized. I believe that one has the responsibility of confessing to a project that it is also done in time in order to avoid creating a lost effort. One point that has also been raised: What does the Court of Auditors bring? I would like to point out that in the context of this review, it has been shown that a savings potential of €44 million has been given for the renovation of the existing route, which, as mentioned above, constitutes €205 million. The ÖBB has already recognised a potential of €25 million as realizable, and it will be implemented accordingly. That is to say, the Court of Auditors has drawn up its own annual budget by means of an audit. I believe that this is a point to which we must point in order to make clear the importance of the Court of Auditors to this Republic. [[Congratulations with the liberals – BZÖ and the ÖVP.]] Another aspect that is very important and which I am particularly addressing, because it also shows that the Court's recommendations have not been implemented with the necessary follow-up, are the external advisory services at ÖBB. In this area, care was not taken with the due diligence of a proper merchant, and the principles of economy, economy and effectiveness were essentially disregarded. Looking at the period from 1999 to 2004, it can be seen that in these years an effort has been made for external consulting services of €90.73 million. This means that the average effort for external consulting services amounted to € 18.73 million in the period 2002 to 2004. This also means that, for example, €6.34 million was spent in 2004 alone for legal advice and €1.2 million for communication services between 2002 and 2004. The Court of Auditors has found serious shortcomings, and it is a question of preventing these serious shortcomings, because it is not reasonable for the taxpayer and you here too to proceed in such a way. I just want to show you some of these mistakes. For example, the contractors formulated their follow-up contracts only after the start of the performance and partly themselves. The consultants already estimated the need for advice or the necessary advisory team after the performance was started. Further: The principles of fair and fair competition have not been observed. On average, the framework contracts were corrected up three times – without adjusting the target values. The excesses of the costs have not been justified or documented. The registration of the extension of the durations was subsequently or retroactively. Written orders were made after performance or after receipt of the corresponding invoices. A daily or personal certificate of performance was waived, although the basis for the billing was. The start of the contract was fictitious to meet the criteria of a follow-up contract. This could be continued at any time – but this alone shows that the necessary care has not been taken. It should also be noted that a consultative firm has been entrusted with a great deal of effort to organize the trips and the performance of the board of directors, while also organising or conducting the performance of the board of directors before its own employees. If you look at the whole thing and you know that there is a permanent organisation process in the ÖBB, if you know that liberalisation steps have to be taken and that at the same time the purchase of Postbus AG has been carried out, you can also see that there is an external advisory effort on the one hand – that is undisputed, I want to stress – but: The way in which this effort was carried out, the reasons for the use of external consultants and the content of the advisory agreements show that the amount of such effort was not justified in any way – and that the effort as such was not justified. It was mentioned here that everything had gotten better: Unfortunately, the information available to the Court of Auditors for 2005 shows that the advisory burden for the entire ÖBB amounted to € 21.34 million, i.e. only € 0.5 million lower than the highest value in 2003. And when you subtract the newly founded companies from the effort of 2005 you can see that the consulting effort at €17.15 million is still high. This is the third highest value since 1994! That is to say, one can certainly return some points of this advisory service to one-off effects, such as IT benefits or, for example, audit costs, nevertheless – and this also shows the information that the Court of Auditors has – that the ÖBB did not proceed with the necessary economy, economy and effectiveness. From the Court of Auditors' point of view, it would be necessary to comply with good governance in this area and to do everything possible to keep the consultation costs efficient in the future, saying: to reduce. Since this is about the award of advisory services, I would also like to say that I agree with what Professor Malik says in his book “The New Corporate Governance”: A member of the Supervisory Board should not be affected by the company in his position of interest, for, according to Professor Malik, it should be the minimum that there is no significant commercial relationship with the company for the time of the Supervisory Board mandate. Furthermore, Professor Malik says that, in addition to a number of other aspects, supervisory councils must be the same or similar to judges, otherwise they will not be able to function effectively. [[Abg. Neudeck: Did he read it himself?]] I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that there is nothing to add to these statements by Professor Malik, and all of us can only hope that what Professor Malik has done will be observed in the future. Thank you.

The aim of the control of illegal employment is to verify compliance with the rules defined in the Foreign Employment Act in the interests of all job-seekers, but also of the entrepreneurs who comply with the legislation. This is done by the nationwide, comprehensive control of the KIAB deployment teams. The control of illegal workers' employment, in short KIAB, has been since 1. ==References====External links== For this purpose, control teams were set up at twelve customs offices. Furthermore, the office of the labour market service and the customs authorities shall have to communicate to the competent authorities if, within the framework of their activities, they arrive at the reasonable suspicion that there is a violation of labour law, social law, health and environmental protection rules. The Court of Auditors reviewed from January to March 2005 the measures taken by the BMF to combat fraud with the focus on employee employment and the so-called rapid intervention group. Ladies and gentlemen! The number of staff rose from originally 88 in 2002 to 178 in 2005 – that is, more than 100 percent. A further increase in staff is planned. In 2004, 23 222 controls were carried out throughout Germany and 6 200 were found illegally employed. These were one third of the construction and construction industries and the gastronomy. Our problem, however, is: We have nine different contacts, and almost every federal has another hardware and software system, so that we cannot use a system from our side, but always need to adapt our system according to the hardware and software there. Point Report of the Financial Committee on the Government Template (1567, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the Act on the Independent Financial Senate, the Standard Consumption Tax Act, the Federal Tax Code and the Soil Estimate Act 1970 – UFSG-Novelle 2006 (1587 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e25176	We reach the 20th. Agenda item. First speaker is Mrs Tamandel. - Please, you're talking.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e25200	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The past history of the independent financial senate is quite a success story, its acceptance in tax practice is remarkable. And if the number of highest court complaints in tax law has recently decreased significantly, this is not only due to the fact that taxpayers no longer fight reflexively against second instance decisions at the Administrative Court. However, experience since the start of the activities of the Independent Financial Senate has also shown that, on the one hand, certain regulations in the Federal Act on the Independent Financial Senate may cause difficulties in the management tasks of the President on the other hand, and the tasks falling within the competence of the General Assembly. This concerns in particular the identification of the inconsistency of activities with the Office a member of the independent financial Senate, the management powers of the heads of the external offices, the evidence of evidence, the controlling and the service period. The fact that the streamlining of management competence will lead to a further increase in efficiency is certainly not to be denied. Furthermore, there are so far no rules on reporting obligations of the main professional members to the President and the President to the Federal Minister of Finance and on the publication of the decisions of the Independent Financial Senate on the Internet. The purpose of this draft law is to create a clear regulation in the areas of problem and to improve the framework conditions, facilitating effective action by the independent financial Senate. Overall, the aim is to increase the concentration of UFS members on legal proceedings and to strengthen the status of the independent financial Senate as an independent management authority. What was once missed among social-democratic finance ministers has been realized with the UFS under Karl-Heinz Grasser, and we want to continue this successful path in favour of the citizens of this country.

Debt especially in the countries of southern Africa is the biggest obstacle to achieving the UN Millennium Development Goals and combating poverty. In most African countries, debt service exceeds the expenditure they can spend on social infrastructure, education, health and others. I would like to give an example: Zambia – one of the world’s highest HIV/AIDS countries – spends two dollars on debt service for every dollar in its health care. The African states alone pay $15 billion annually to the north. That means: Debt is a very important means, as my previous speaker has already said, to release other, additional funds and invest in social infrastructure. The recovery of funds for IDA and for the African Development Fund is undoubtedly useful. In order to ensure the immediate, complete and irrevocable repayment under the multilateral decommitment initiatives, these commitments should also be actually granted for the entire period of the period. I would also like to stress that, for example, this also concerns multilateral decommissioning measures by the Inter-American Development Bank. ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e28595	Mr President. For years parents have been demanding more safety for children in school buses and at least for as long as experts point to the unsatisfying and dangerous legal situation in the field of the transport of children in school buses. The so-called 3:2 counting rule in the Motor Driving Act leads three children under the age of 14 to count as two persons. The consequence of the current legal situation in practice: Schoolchildren push, packed with heavy school bags, in overcrowded buses and then have to share a two-seat seat with thirds of school bags – and that without safety belt! 6 747 people in Burgenland and Styria have signed in recent months for more safety on the school road – in concrete terms: a seat and a safety belt per child –. These initiatives were launched last week. The Committee on Petitions and the hope that the government will finally become active and that from the next school year thousands of children will be able to go to school more safely, especially from rural areas, has been disappointed, as my previous speaker explained. Instead of finally acting, ÖVP and BZÖ have just forwarded these initiatives to the Transport Committee, probably knowing that this will no longer be dealt with before the National Council elections. The legislator is well aware of the problem. It is mentioned on a regular basis. Opportunities to introduce the 1:1 counting rule for all trips on the school bus would have been enough in the past six years, but this federal government has unfortunately allowed all opportunities to pass. The attitude on the safety of schoolchildren in transport is very inconsistent: On the one hand, the legislator requires the private driver to provide a seat per child, and this must also be secured accordingly, but on the other hand, thousands of children are not properly transported in daily public transport. I should therefore like to ask that this law be adopted during the next legislative period. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e28790	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to make one more brief comment on the motion for a resolution (Doc. 73 on mistletoe therapy. It should be noted that 30 percent of cancer patients take part in complementary medical treatment, using the mistletoes both supportively and palliatively alone or complementaryly with other treatments. Mistel therapy is the most commonly prescribed complementary medical therapy in Central Europe; 40 to 60 percent of oncological patients receive it. The benefits are secured in over 70 scientific studies: fewer side effects due to chemotherapy and radiation therapy, less pain. Patients need less accompanying medication during chemotherapy and can even contribute to therapy. It should also be noted that a brightening effect is observed. The therapy is usually started after surgery and after the initiation of chemotherapy or radiation therapy. Mrs. Machne has already pointed out that self-administration in social security in certain areas, even if scientific evidence has not been provided, is very likely to rely on the experience of the treating doctors and to allow the insured who are still without sufficient evidence access to therapy. In this case, this means that the mistletoe therapy can be prescribed additively to the oncological treatment concept by the treating doctor, if he considers it necessary. The resulting costs are taken over by social security; the patient is not charged with this. I hope that's what's going on in all the health insurance companies. Thank you.

On weekends and holidays there are sometimes no links at all – and this in a labour market problem region. All the signs indicate that public transport is to be shifted away from the railway to the road – and that in the Hohe Tauern National Park region with 1.4 million nights and thousands of daily guests. The Federal Government, through the lack of transport policy and the pressure on public transport, contributes to the fact that the overall priorities are misplaced and too little investment is being made in public transport. The Federal Government's plans to shift responsibility for public transport to the federal states that are forced into an insoluble financial situation, which also affects the municipalities, will result in further savings. The petition with more than 3,000 signatures is a call for help from the people we should take seriously. The opinion of BMVIT will be such that the development and maintenance of public transport is not within its competence. Mr Scheuch, I would like to invite you to take me to the Pinzgau-Pongau-Lungau region, and then you will experience that a disabled person who comes to the training centre in Oberrain is on the road for a distance of 40 kilometres for five hours.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-13-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00160_d8e29268	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to focus briefly on the subject of mistletoe therapy. The mistletoe has a tradition as a healing plant for centuries. She was led as a healing plant in ancient times and in the Middle Ages. In particular, the therapeutic efficacy of complementary cancer therapy has been seen in relation to the improved quality of life for oncological patients scientifically confirmed several times. Unfortunately, some 32,000 people in Austria suffer from cancer each year; 20,000 die of this disease. About two thirds of cancer patients receive complementary treatment for radiation and chemotherapy, for example with mistletoe preparations. ==References== April 2006 will now be financed under certain conditions in Austria. That means that the patient is not taxed financially. The prerequisites are: additive in individual cases for oncological therapy and then, if oncological therapy remains without effect or is no longer possible. As early as January 2005, the Lower Austrian regional health insurance fund has decided to replace the costs of mistletoe therapy after the approval of the chief physician. It is currently under consideration whether or not mistletoe therapy should generally be included in the refund code. I think that the mistletoe therapy for the oncological patient is proven to be an improvement in the quality of life or an additional opportunity and, above all, additional hope.
